## Appendix C - Apple Liquid Glass: Honest Web Approximation

Do **not** treat random CSS snippets as official Apple Liquid Glass.

### What is official
Apple documents Liquid Glass inside Apple's Human Interface Guidelines and Developer Documentation for **Apple platforms**. It is a dynamic material used across Apple platform UI. Apple's native implementation belongs to Apple platform APIs and system components, **not a public web CSS package**.

Relevant official docs:
- Apple Human Interface Guidelines → Materials
- Apple Developer Documentation → Liquid Glass
- Apple Developer Documentation → Adopting Liquid Glass
- SwiftUI → Material

### What is NOT official
There is no `liquid-glass.css` from Apple for normal websites.

A web approximation can use:
- `backdrop-filter`
- transparent backgrounds
- layered borders
- highlight overlays
- gradients
- motion
- strong contrast fallbacks

But that is **web glassmorphism / frosted-glass approximation**, not official Apple Liquid Glass. Label it as such in comments.

### Safer web approximation skeleton

```css
.liquid-glass-web-approx {
  position: relative;
  isolation: isolate;
  overflow: hidden;
  border-radius: 999px;
  border: 1px solid rgb(255 255 255 / .32);
  background:
    linear-gradient(135deg, rgb(255 255 255 / .30), rgb(255 255 255 / .08)),
    rgb(255 255 255 / .12);
  backdrop-filter: blur(24px) saturate(180%) contrast(1.05);
  -webkit-backdrop-filter: blur(24px) saturate(180%) contrast(1.05);
  box-shadow:
    inset 0 1px 0 rgb(255 255 255 / .48),
    inset 0 -1px 0 rgb(255 255 255 / .12),
    0 18px 60px rgb(0 0 0 / .18);
}

.liquid-glass-web-approx::before {
  content: "";
  position: absolute;
  inset: 0;
  z-index: -1;
  border-radius: inherit;
  background:
    radial-gradient(circle at 20% 0%, rgb(255 255 255 / .55), transparent 34%),
    linear-gradient(90deg, rgb(255 255 255 / .18), transparent 42%, rgb(255 255 255 / .14));
  pointer-events: none;
}

.liquid-glass-web-approx::after {
  content: "";
  position: absolute;
  inset: 1px;
  border-radius: inherit;
  border: 1px solid rgb(255 255 255 / .14);
  pointer-events: none;
}

@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) {
  .liquid-glass-web-approx {
    border-color: rgb(255 255 255 / .18);
    background:
      linear-gradient(135deg, rgb(255 255 255 / .16), rgb(255 255 255 / .04)),
      rgb(15 23 42 / .42);
    box-shadow:
      inset 0 1px 0 rgb(255 255 255 / .22),
      0 18px 60px rgb(0 0 0 / .42);
  }
}

@media (prefers-reduced-transparency: reduce) {
  .liquid-glass-web-approx {
    background: rgb(255 255 255 / .96);
    backdrop-filter: none;
    -webkit-backdrop-filter: none;
  }
}
```

**Important:** `prefers-reduced-transparency` has uneven browser support; test it. Always provide enough contrast even without blur.

def _clip(text: Optional[str], limit: int = PREVIEW_CHARS) -> Optional[str]:
    if text is None:
        return None
    return text if len(text) <= limit else text[:limit] + "... [truncated]"


def evidence_payload(ev: Evidence) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    data = ev.model_dump(mode="json", exclude={"raw_reference", "scan_id"})
    data["description"] = _clip(data.get("description"))
    return data


class RunRecorder:
    def __init__(self, ledger: InvestigationLedger, run_id: str) -> None:
        self.ledger = ledger
        self.run_id = run_id

    async def _append(self, event_type: EventType, payload: Dict[str, Any], causation_id: Optional[str] = None) -> LedgerEvent:
        return await self.ledger.append(self.run_id, event_type, payload, causation_id=causation_id)

    async def run_started(self, payload: Dict[str, Any]) -> LedgerEvent:
        return await self._append(EventType.RUN_STARTED, payload)

    async def artifact_ingested(self, artifact: Any, causation_id: str) -> LedgerEvent:
        return await self._append(EventType.ARTIFACT_INGESTED, _artifact_payload(artifact), causation_id)

    async def model_called(self, payload: Dict[str, Any], causation_id: Optional[str]) -> LedgerEvent:
        payload = dict(payload)
        payload["content"] = _clip(payload.get("content"))
        payload["reasoning_content"] = _clip(payload.get("reasoning_content"))
        return await self._append(EventType.MODEL_CALLED, payload, causation_id)

    async def tool_called(self, call_id: str, tool_name: str, arguments: Dict[str, Any], causation_id: str) -> LedgerEvent:
        return await self._append(EventType.TOOL_CALLED, {"tool_call_id": call_id, "tool": tool_name, "arguments": arguments}, causation_id)

    async def execution_completed(self, record: ExecutionRecord, summary: str, causation_id: str) -> LedgerEvent:
        payload = record.model_dump(mode="json")
        payload["summary"] = _clip(summary, 600)
        return await self._append(EventType.EXECUTION_COMPLETED, payload, causation_id)

    async def artifact_created(self, artifact: Any, causation_id: str) -> LedgerEvent:
        return await self._append(EventType.ARTIFACT_CREATED, _artifact_payload(artifact), causation_id)

    async def evidence_asserted(self, ev: Evidence, causation_id: Optional[str], baseline: bool = False) -> LedgerEvent:
        if not baseline:
            missing = [f for f in ("source_artifact_id", "source_execution_id", "source_tool_call_id") if not getattr(ev, f)]
            if missing:
                raise LedgerProvenanceError(f"Evidence {ev.id} ({ev.type}) lacks provenance: {', '.join(missing)}")
        payload = evidence_payload(ev)
        payload["baseline"] = baseline
        return await self._append(EventType.EVIDENCE_ASSERTED, payload, causation_id)

    async def hypothesis_proposed(self, hyp: Any, causation_id: Optional[str]) -> LedgerEvent:
        return await self._append(EventType.HYPOTHESIS_PROPOSED, {
            "hypothesis_id": hyp.id, "claim_type": hyp.claim_type, "claim_summary": hyp.claim_summary,
            "target": hyp.target_identifier, "status": hyp.epistemic_status.value,
        }, causation_id)

    async def hypothesis_transitioned(self, payload: Dict[str, Any], causation_id: Optional[str]) -> LedgerEvent:
        return await self._append(EventType.HYPOTHESIS_TRANSITIONED, payload, causation_id)

    async def supervisor_decision(self, decision_type: str, action: str, details: Dict[str, Any],
                                  causation_id: Optional[str]) -> LedgerEvent:
        return await self._append(EventType.SUPERVISOR_DECISION,
                                  {"decision_type": decision_type, "action": action, "details": details}, causation_id)

    async def run_finalized(self, payload: Dict[str, Any], causation_id: Optional[str]) -> LedgerEvent:
        return await self._append(EventType.RUN_FINALIZED, payload, causation_id)


def _artifact_payload(artifact: Any) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    return {
        "artifact_id": artifact.artifact_id,
        "handle": artifact.handle,
        "sha256": artifact.sha256,
        "size_bytes": artifact.size_bytes,
        "artifact_type": artifact.artifact_type,
        "mime_type": artifact.mime_type,
        "storage_uri": artifact.storage_uri,
        "parent_artifact_ids": list(artifact.parent_artifact_ids),
        "produced_by_execution_id": artifact.produced_by_execution_id,
        "produced_by_tool_call_id": artifact.produced_by_tool_call_id,
        "producer": artifact.producer,
        "producer_version": artifact.producer_version,
    }

No .claude/launch.json found. Create /home/p4cketsn1ff3r/Projects/Active/KAVACH_AI/.claude/launch.json with this format:
{
  "version": "0.0.1",
  "configurations": [
    {
      "name": "<unique-name>",
      "runtimeExecutable": "<command>",
      "runtimeArgs": ["<args>"],
      "port": <port>
    }
  ]
}
Set "runtimeExecutable" to the command (e.g. "npm"), "runtimeArgs" to the arguments (e.g. ["run", "dev"]), and "port" to the server port. An optional "url" (http/https) opens the preview there instead of http://localhost:<port>. A localhost "url" must be just the server's origin — no path or query, matching the entry's port — for example "https://localhost:8443" or "http://app.localhost:3000"; to show a specific page, navigate after the preview opens. Non-localhost URLs may carry paths and are subject to the user's permission and the organization's browsing policy. A configuration with "url" and no command attaches to an already-running server. Only include servers you actually need to preview. Then call preview_start with the server name.

from system.toolchain import try_resolve_tool

logger = logging.getLogger("kavach.intelligence.llama_launcher")

@dataclass
class LlamaLauncherConfig:
    # None = resolve via system.toolchain (KAVACH_LLAMA_SERVER_PATH / KAVACH_MODEL_PATH, PATH, appliance).
    binary_path: Optional[str] = None
    model_path: Optional[str] = None
    host: str = "127.0.0.1"
    port: int = 8089
    ctx_size: int = 4096
    n_gpu_layers: int = 8
    threads: int = 4
    api_key: str = "local-no-key"
    device: Optional[str] = "CUDA0"
    cache_prompt: bool = True
    slots: int = 1
    flash_attn: Optional[str] = "auto"


def is_port_in_use(port: int, host: str = "127.0.0.1") -> bool:
    """Check if a TCP port is currently occupied."""
    with socket.socket(socket.AF_INET, socket.SOCK_STREAM) as s:
        s.settimeout(0.5)
        return s.connect_ex((host, port)) == 0


def find_available_port(start_port: int = 8089, max_attempts: int = 20) -> int:
    """Find the first unoccupied port starting from start_port."""
    for p in range(start_port, start_port + max_attempts):
        if not is_port_in_use(p):
            return p
    raise RuntimeError(f"No available ports found in range {start_port}..{start_port + max_attempts}")


class LlamaLauncher:

--- [Step 161 | THINKING] ---
With a 4096 token context window, I need to reserve about 512 tokens for output and margin, leaving roughly 3500 for input. I'm sketching a context-budget method in the controller that trims older tool-role messages down to short stubs whenever the estimated prompt size exceeds the window minus reserve, using the gateway's token estimator to decide when to elide.

<h1 align="center">🛡️ KAVACH AI (कवच AI) 2.0</h1>

<p align="center">
  <strong>Evidence-driven forensic investigation platform for Android applications</strong>
</p>

---

> [!WARNING]
> **Status: under active development, not yet a runnable product.**
> KAVACH 2.0 currently consists of tested components without an API or worker entrypoint.
> The KAVACH 1.x application (FastAPI + Gemini + MobSF/Frida dashboard) has been retired from this branch;
> its final state is preserved at the git tag [`v1-final`](https://github.com/RamNarra/KAVACH-AI/tree/v1-final).

---

## What exists today

| Component | Location | State |
|---|---|---|
| Static analysis DAG orchestrator | `backend/orchestration/`, `backend/analyzers/` | Androguard, APKiD, manifest, MobSF, Quark, YARA-X analyzers run concurrently with per-stage timeouts |
| Deterministic risk policy & report | `backend/risk/policy_engine.py`, `backend/intelligence/deterministic_reporter.py` | YAML-driven policy, deterministic report generation |
| Canonical persistence | `backend/persistence/`, `backend/alembic/` | SQLAlchemy 2 async + PostgreSQL: samples, runs, stage executions, evidence, relationships, risk, reports, artifacts |
| Investigation controller | `backend/agent/` | Multi-turn tool-calling loop with supervisor budget, repetition, diminishing-returns and frontier-gate checks; claim-specific hypothesis adjudication (`agent/adjudication.py`) |
| Model gateway | `backend/intelligence/` | Provider abstraction over an OpenAI-compatible `llama-server`; requires an explicitly resolved model profile; mock provider for tests |
| Execution broker & sandbox | `backend/execution/`, `backend/workbench/` | Policy-escalated isolation tiers; bubblewrap-hardened out-of-process execution that fails closed |
| Forensic tools | `backend/tools/` | Container repair, payload decoding, string/xref search, JADX, ELF symbols. Separate investigation and self-repair registries; schema-invalid calls are rejected before execution; artifacts are addressed only by opaque `artifact://` handles; JADX and readelf run through the ExecutionBroker sandbox; the model sees only a sanitized, size-bounded `ToolObservation` marked as untrusted data |
| Runtime toolchain | `backend/system/toolchain.py` | Resolves llama-server, model, JADX, APKTool, YARA, Java, adb from `KAVACH_*_PATH`, then `PATH`, then appliance paths |

## Known limitations (tracked for the next phases)

- No API/worker entrypoint wires the orchestrator, controller and persistence together.
- Artifact handles resolve to per-run host paths and external tools read staged copies (no ArtifactStore yet). The in-process tools (container repair, payload decoding, string/xref/payload search) parse hostile bytes inside the KAVACH process.
- Investigation state (hypotheses, tool calls, model calls, execution manifests) is not yet persisted to the database; evidence has no typed provenance links yet (see `docs/provenance-requirements.md`).
- The agent protocol still replays tool calls as `[Actions ...]` assistant text and observations as user messages, not native `tool` messages. The live probe (`test_native_tool_compatibility_probe.py`, MODEL suite) characterizes native tool calling of the provisioned llama-server + GGUF; the controller has not been migrated.
- Context overflow is only guarded (output clamp); there is no ContextAssembler.
- `backend/evals/agent_eval.py` is diagnostic telemetry and not suitable for model comparison.
- `evals/run_evals.py` scores hand-authored responses; it is a synthetic fixture, **not** an empirical measurement of KAVACH.
- `frontend/` targets the retired 1.x API and is unsupported until a 2.0 API exists.
- The appliance build is only partially reproducible: base image, llama.cpp images, JADX, APKTool and the GGUF are pinned by digest/SHA-256, but Python requirements use version floors (no lockfile) and apt packages are unpinned.

## Directory layout

```
backend/
  agent/           investigation controller, supervisor, prompt builder
  analyzers/       static analyzer plugins
  domain/          canonical domain models (evidence, run, investigation, trace, ...)
  execution/       ExecutionBroker, policy, backend resolver, bwrap backend
  intelligence/    model gateway, providers, model profiles, deterministic reporter
  orchestration/   async static-analysis DAG
  persistence/     database engine, ORM models, persistence service
  system/          host doctor, resource governor, runtime toolchain resolution
  risk/            deterministic risk policy engine
  tools/           forensic tool registry and tools
  workbench/       sandbox executor, filesystem domains, policy
  rules/           YARA rules and behaviour catalog
deploy/            appliance Dockerfile, compose (PostgreSQL + llama-server) and dev override
docs/              design notes (provenance requirements)
frontend/          unsupported (1.x UI)
scripts/           setup and verification scripts
```

## Development

```bash
./scripts/setup.sh                              # create backend venv, install requirements (Python only;
                                                # --with-frontend also installs the unsupported 1.x UI)
(cd backend && venv/bin/python -m system.doctor) # check host toolchain (bwrap, jadx, models, ...)
./scripts/verify_all.sh                         # run the backend verification gate
```

`verify_all.sh` reports each suite separately:

| Suite | Needs | Default |
|---|---|---|
| UNIT, CONTRACT | Python deps only (synthetic, benign fixtures) | required |
| SECURITY | bubblewrap >= 0.12 in an approved root (`/opt/kavach/runtime/bin`, `/usr/bin`, ...) | required |
| INTEGRATION | PostgreSQL (`POSTGRES_*`), Docker Compose CLI; includes `alembic check` (zero drift, unknown tables flagged) | required |
| REPAIR | git (developer self-repair worktree tooling; disposable clone) | required |
| ACCEPTANCE | private APK corpus via `KAVACH_ACCEPTANCE_FIXTURES` (never committed) | NOT RUN unless set |
| MODEL | `KAVACH_RUN_MODEL_SUITE=1`, `KAVACH_LLAMA_SERVER_PATH`, `KAVACH_MODEL_PATH` (optional `KAVACH_MODEL_SHA256`, `KAVACH_PROBE_GPU_LAYERS`, `KAVACH_PROBE_DEVICE`) | NOT RUN unless set |

A skipped test in a required suite fails the gate; it is never counted as a pass.

### Appliance compose

`deploy/docker-compose.appliance.yml` keeps PostgreSQL and llama-server on an internal network with no
published ports, and reads the database password from a Compose secret
(`deploy/secrets/postgres_password` or `KAVACH_POSTGRES_PASSWORD_FILE`). For host access during
development add `-f deploy/docker-compose.dev.yml` (binds 127.0.0.1 only). A one-shot `model-verify`
service checks the GGUF against `KAVACH_MODEL_SHA256` before either llama-server starts.

### Phase 1 Invariants & Boundaries

- **Hostile-byte parser boundary**: `InvestigationService` must not be exposed as a production-facing API until in-process hostile-byte parsers have explicit size/resource bounds or have been moved behind the execution boundary.
- **Model identity verification**: Verifying that the actually loaded and served inference model SHA-256 matches the configured model profile digest is a mandatory admission gate before admitting runs.

**Local commits (7 ahead of `origin/master`):** your three earlier ones (`9390574`, `25c5833`, `123d610`) plus `32e0c8e`, `ee3f49a`, `78d39d0`. The working tree is clean.

**The 6 fixes:**
1. **Run status:** a run is now QUEUED, then RUNNING, then one of COMPLETED, COMPLETED_NO_FINDINGS, PARTIAL, FAILED, TIMEOUT or INTERRUPTED. The run row, the API and the ledger's final event now record the same final status. Unknown or unfinished states raise an error instead of being turned into FAILED. A test covers every final state.
2. **Upload ordering:** the run is created as QUEUED, then the APK is stored, then the investigation starts. If storing fails, the run is closed as FAILED in both the ledger and the run row, and the upload returns an error with the `run_id`. I also added:
   - a time limit per run, which records TIMEOUT;
   - shutdown records INTERRUPTED;
   - on startup, runs left open by a crash are closed, and run rows that disagree with the ledger are corrected.
3. **Dependencies:** `fastapi`, `uvicorn` and `python-multipart` are now in `requirements.txt`.
4. **Clean environment:** a fresh Python 3.12 environment built only from `requirements.txt` imports the service and passes the tests.
5. **Native tool calls:** the agent now sends the model's tool calls back in the standard tool-call format, and each call gets one reply. The old `[Actions ...]` text is gone. Reading tool calls out of plain text only happens if you switch on `legacy_text_tool_calls=True`. When a prompt would overflow the 4096-token window, the oldest tool results are shortened; full results stay in the ledger.
6. **Demo fixture:** `service/demo_fixture.py` builds a harmless APK. It has a real binary manifest (package `com.kavach.demo.fixture`, INTERNET and READ_SMS permissions) and one file falsely marked as encrypted. Triage proposes a CONTAINER_EVASION hypothesis. The fixture is described as non-malware everywhere.

**Also built:**
- **Console:** `service/console/index.html` is one file with no external downloads. It shows the live timeline, hypotheses, evidence, artifact lineage, the report, and a "LEDGER VERIFIED" panel with the head hash. I checked it in the browser preview and there were no errors.
- **Production startup:** `service/main.py` connects everything to Postgres; the run command is in the README. `run_jadx` is only offered to the model when JADX is installed, and it isn't on this machine.
- **README:** updated to describe the demo slice and its current limits.

**Live run with Qwen3.5-4B (memory storage, real model):** the model chose `inspect_container`, then `repair_container`. The hypothesis was confirmed only after the repair succeeded, not after the inspection. The model then concluded, the run ended COMPLETED, and the ledger verified, in 76 seconds.

The first live attempt ended PARTIAL: the model kept running `search_strings` until the stop for unproductive turns kicked in. To fix it, the supervisor now sends the model one message once every hypothesis is resolved. This is guidance only; the existing finalization check still decides whether the run can end.

The last model reply hit the 256-token limit and was cut short, so the conclusion text may end mid-sentence on screen.
